\u7b2c54\u7ae0\uff1aLlamaIndex\u6838\u5fc3\u67b6\u6784\u5256\u6790\n\n### Node/Document\u62bd\u8c61\u5c42\u6784\u5efa\u4e86RAG\u7684\u6570\u636e\u57fa\u77f3\n\nLlamaIndex\u7684\u6570\u636e\u6a21\u578b\u4ee5`BaseNode`\u4e3a\u6838\u5fc3\uff0c\u6784\u5efa\u4e86\u4e00\u5957\u652f\u6301\u591a\u6a21\u6001\u3001\u5c42\u6b21\u5316\u5173\u7cfb\u7684\u8282\u70b9\u4f53\u7cfb\u3002\u6e90\u4ee3\u7801\u4f4d\u4e8e`llama-index-core/llama_index/core/schema.py`\uff0c\u7c7b\u7ee7\u627f\u4f53\u7cfb\u5982\u4e0b\uff1a\n\n```\nBaseComponent        \u2514\u2500\u2500 TextNode (\u6587\u672c\u8282\u70b9 - \u5411\u540e\u517c\u5bb9)\n                    \u251c\u2500\u2500 ImageNode\n                    \u2514\u2500\u2500 IndexNode\n```\n\n**\u8282\u70b9ID\u751f\u6210\u91c7\u7528UUID\u7b56\u7565**\uff0c\u9ed8\u8ba4\u4f7f\u7528`uuid.uuid4()`\u751f\u6210\u552f\u4e00\u6807\u8bc6\u7b26\u3002\u54c8\u5e0c\u8ba1\u7b97\u5219\u57fa\u4e8e\u5185\u5bb9\u548c\u5143\u6570\u636e\u7684SHA-256\u6458\u8981\uff0c`TextNode.hash`\u5c5e\u6027\u901a\u8fc7`sha256(str(self.text) + str(self.metadata))`\u5b9e\u73b0\u5185\u5bb9\u6307\u7eb9\u3002\u8fd9\u4e00\u8bbe\u8ba1\u652f\u6301IngestionPipeline\u7684**\u589e\u91cf\u66f4\u65b0\u68c0\u6d4b**\u2014\u2014\u5f53\u6587\u6863\u5185\u5bb9\u53d8\u5316\u65f6\u54c8\u5e0c\u6539\u53d8\uff0c\u89e6\u53d1\u91cd\u65b0\u5904\u7406\u3002\n\n\u8282\u70b9\u5173\u7cfb\u901a\u8fc7`NodeRelationship`\u679a\u4e3e\u5efa\u6a21\uff0c\u652f\u6301\u4e94\u79cd\u5173\u7cfb\u7c7b\u578b\uff1a**SOURCE**\uff08\u6e90\u6587\u6863\uff09\u3001**PREVIOUS/NEXT**\uff08\u524d\u540e\u5e8f\u8282\u70b9\uff09\u3001**PARENT/CHILD**\uff08\u5c42\u6b21\u7ed3\u6784\uff09\u3002\u8fd9\u79cd\u8bbe\u8ba1\u4f7f\u5f97`AutoMergingRetriever`\u548c`RecursiveRetriever`\u80fd\u591f\u5728\u68c0\u7d22\u65f6\u904d\u5386\u8282\u70b9\u56fe\u7ed3\u6784\u3002\u5143\u6570\u636e\u7ba1\u7406\u673a\u5236\u63d0\u4f9b\u4e86\u7ec6\u7c92\u5ea6\u63a7\u5236\uff0c`excluded_embed_metadata_keys`\u548c`excluded_llm_metadata_keys`\u5206\u522b\u63a7\u5236\u5d4c\u5165\u548cLLM\u63a8\u7406\u65f6\u5305\u542b\u7684\u5143\u6570\u636e\u5b57\u6bb5\u3002\n\n### ServiceContext\u5230Settings\u7684\u67b6\u6784\u6f14\u8fdb\u89e3\u51b3\u4e86\u914d\u7f6e\u75db\u70b9\n\n**\u7248\u672c\u65f6\u95f4\u7ebf**\uff1av0.10.0\uff082024.02\uff09\u5f15\u5165Settings\u5355\u4f8b\u5e76\u6807\u8bb0ServiceContext\u5f03\u7528\uff1bv0.11.0\uff082024.08\uff09\u5b8c\u5168\u79fb\u9664ServiceContext\uff0c\u4f7f\u7528\u4f1a\u76f4\u63a5\u62a5\u9519\u3002\n\nServiceContext\u7684\u6838\u5fc3\u95ee\u9898\u5728\u4e8e**\u5f3a\u5236\u52a0\u8f7d**\u548c**\u4f7f\u7528\u4e0d\u4e00\u81f4**\u3002\u521b\u5efaServiceContext\u65f6\u4f1a\u52a0\u8f7d\u6240\u6709\u7ec4\u4ef6\u5230\u5185\u5b58\uff08LLM\u3001Embedding\u6a21\u578b\u3001NodeParser\u7b49\uff09\uff0c\u5373\u4f7f\u540e\u7eed\u672a\u4f7f\u7528\uff1b\u540c\u65f6\u6846\u67b6\u5185\u90e8\u8bb8\u591a\u6a21\u5757\u672a\u6b63\u786e\u4f20\u9012ServiceContext\uff0c\u5bfc\u81f4\u914d\u7f6e\u5931\u6548\u3002Settings\u5355\u4f8b\u6a21\u5f0f\uff08\u6e90\u7801\u8def\u5f84`llama-index-core/llama_index/core/settings.py`\uff09\u901a\u8fc7**\u61d2\u52a0\u8f7d**\u89e3\u51b3\u4e86\u8fd9\u4e9b\u95ee\u9898\uff1a\n\n```python\nfrom \u7d22\u5f15\u57fa\u7c7b\u7ee7\u627f\u4f53\u7cfb\u652f\u6491\u591a\u6837\u5316\u68c0\u7d22\u7b56\u7565\n\n`BaseIndex`\uff08\u6e90\u7801`llama-index-core/llama_index/core/indices/base.py`\uff09\u4f5c\u4e3a\u6cdb\u578b\u62bd\u8c61\u57fa\u7c7b\uff0c\u5b9a\u4e49\u4e86\u7d22\u5f15\u7684\u6838\u5fc3\u5951\u7ea6\uff1a\n\n```python\nclass IngestionPipeline\u5b9e\u73b0\u4e86\u53ef\u7f13\u5b58\u7684\u6570\u636e\u6444\u5165\u6d41\u6c34\u7ebf\n\n\u6e90\u7801\u8def\u5f84`llama-index-core/llama_index/core/ingestion/pipeline.py`\uff0c\u91c7\u7528**\u7ba1\u9053\u8fc7\u6ee4\u5668\u6a21\u5f0f**\uff1a\n\n```python\npipeline BaseRetriever\u63a5\u53e3\u8bbe\u8ba1\u5b9e\u73b0\u4e86\u68c0\u7d22\u7b56\u7565\u7684\u7075\u6d3b\u7ec4\u5408\n\n\u6e90\u7801\u8def\u5f84`llama-index-core/llama_index/core/base/base_retriever.py`\uff0c\u6838\u5fc3\u65b9\u6cd5\u7b7e\u540d\uff1a\n\n```python\nclass BaseRetriever(PromptMixin, DispatcherSpanMixin):\n    def retrieve(str_or_query_bundle: QueryType) -> List[NodeWithScore]: ...\n    def _retrieve(self, query_bundle: QueryBundle) -> List[NodeWithScore]: ...  # \u5b50\u7c7b\u5b9e\u73b0\n    async def aretrieve(str_or_query_bundle: QueryType) -> List[NodeWithScore]: ...\n```\n\n**\u4e94\u79cd\u5173\u952e\u68c0\u7d22\u5668\u5b9e\u73b0**\uff1a\n\n1. **VectorIndexRetriever**\uff1a\u6807\u51c6\u5411\u91cf\u76f8\u4f3c\u5ea6\u68c0\u7d22\uff0c`similarity_top_k`\u63a7\u5236\u8fd4\u56de\u6570\u91cf\n2. **RouterRetriever**\uff1a\u4f7f\u7528LLM\u52a8\u6001\u9009\u62e9\u6700\u9002\u5408\u7684\u68c0\u7d22\u5668\uff0c\u652f\u6301`select_multi=True`\u805a\u5408\u591a\u4e2a\u7ed3\u679c\n3. **RecursiveRetriever**\uff1a\u5c42\u6b21\u5316\u6570\u636e\u7684\u9012\u5f52\u68c0\u7d22\uff0c\u53ef\u94fe\u63a5SQL/Pandas\u67e5\u8be2\u5f15\u64ce\n4. **AutoMergingRetriever**\uff1a\u68c0\u7d22\u53f6\u5b50\u8282\u70b9\u540e\u81ea\u52a8\u5408\u5e76\u4e3a\u7236\u8282\u70b9\u4e0a\u4e0b\u6587\n5. **QueryFusionRetriever**\uff1a\u591a\u68c0\u7d22\u5668\u7ed3\u679c\u878d\u5408\uff0c\u652f\u6301\u67e5\u8be2\u53d8\u4f53\u751f\u6210\n\n### \u6df7\u5408\u68c0\u7d22\u901a\u8fc7\u5012\u6570\u6392\u540d\u878d\u5408\u5b9e\u73b0\u6700\u4f73\u53ec\u56de\n\n\u6e90\u7801\u8def\u5f84`llama-index-core/llama_index/core/retrievers/fusion_retriever.py`\uff0c\u6838\u5fc3\u7b97\u6cd5\u4e3a**Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF)**\uff1a\n\n```python\ndef reciprocal_rank_fusion(results_list, k=60):\n    fused_scores = {}\n    for results in results_list:\n        for rank, node in enumerate(results):\n            fused_scores[node.id] = fused_scores.get(node.id, 0) + 1.0 / (rank + k)\n    return sorted(fused_scores.items(), key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n```\n\n\u53c2\u6570`k`\u63a7\u5236\u5f02\u5e38\u6392\u540d\u7684\u5f71\u54cd\uff0c\u9ed8\u8ba460\u3002\u878d\u5408\u6a21\u5f0f\u652f\u6301`reciprocal_rerank`\uff08RRF\uff09\u3001`simple`\uff08\u7b80\u5355\u5408\u5e76\uff09\u3001`dist_based`\uff08\u57fa\u4e8e\u5206\u5e03\uff09\u3002\u5178\u578b\u914d\u7f6e\u4e3aBM25+Vector\u6df7\u5408\uff1a\u521d\u59cb\u68c0\u7d22`similarity_top_k=10-20`\uff0c\u901a\u8fc7\u91cd\u6392\u5e8f\u5668\u7b5b\u9009\u5230\u6700\u7ec82-5\u4e2a\u7ed3\u679c\u3002\n\n### \u91cd\u6392\u5e8f\u5668\u901a\u8fc7NodePostprocessor\u63a5\u53e3\u5b9e\u73b0\u5373\u63d2\u5373\u7528\n\n\u6e90\u7801\u8def\u5f84`llama-index-core/llama_index/core/postprocessor/`\uff0c\u63d0\u4f9b\u7edf\u4e00\u63a5\u53e3`postprocess_nodes(nodes, query_bundle)`\u3002\u4e3b\u8981\u5b9e\u73b0\u5305\u62ec\uff1a\n\n| \u91cd\u6392\u5e8f\u5668 | \u673a\u5236 | \u6027\u80fd\u7279\u70b9 |\n|----------|------|----------|\n| **CohereRerank** | Cohere API\u8bed\u4e49\u8bc4\u4f30 | \u9ad8\u8d28\u91cf\uff0c\u9700API\u8c03\u7528 |\n| **SentenceTransformerRerank** | CrossEncoder\u672c\u5730\u63a8\u7406 | \u5e73\u8861\u901f\u5ea6\u4e0e\u51c6\u786e\u5ea6 \u7ec6\u7c92\u5ea6\u5339\u914d \u7b2c56\u7ae0\uff1aWorkflows\u4e8b\u4ef6\u9a71\u52a8\u67b6\u6784\n\n### 2024\u5e748\u6708Workflows\u53d1\u5e03\u89e3\u51b3\u4e86DAG\u67b6\u6784\u7684\u6839\u672c\u5c40\u9650\n\nLlamaIndex\u57282024\u5e74\u521d\u5f15\u5165Query Pipelines\u4f5c\u4e3a\u58f0\u660e\u5f0f\u7f16\u6392API\uff0c\u4f46\u5728\u6dfb\u52a0\u5faa\u73af\u652f\u6301\u65f6\u9047\u5230**DAG\u4e0d\u53ef\u903e\u8d8a\u7684\u9650\u5236**\uff1a\u6709\u5411\u65e0\u73af\u56fe\u7684\"\u65e0\u73af\"\u7279\u6027\u4f7f\u5176\u65e0\u6cd5\u539f\u751f\u652f\u6301Agent\u5faa\u73af\u573a\u666f\u3002\u6b64\u5916\uff0c\u8fb9\u903b\u8f91\u590d\u6742\u3001\u6570\u636e\u4f20\u9012\u56f0\u96be\u3001\u5f00\u53d1\u4f53\u9a8c\u4e0d\u81ea\u7136\u7b49\u95ee\u9898\u4fc3\u4f7f\u56e2\u961f\u91cd\u65b0\u8bbe\u8ba1\u3002\n\n**2024\u5e748\u6708\u6b63\u5f0f\u53d1\u5e03Workflows**\uff0c\u91c7\u7528\u4e8b\u4ef6\u9a71\u52a8\u67b6\u6784\u66ff\u4ee3\u56fe\u904d\u5386\u3002\u6838\u5fc3\u5dee\u5f02\u5728\u4e8e**\u63a7\u5236\u6743\u8f6c\u79fb**\uff1aDAG\u4e2d\u6846\u67b6\u51b3\u5b9a\u4e0b\u4e00\u4e2a\u7ec4\u4ef6\u548c\u6570\u636e\u4f20\u9012\uff08\"\u9ed1\u76d2\"\u95ee\u9898\uff09\uff0cWorkflows\u4e2d\u5f00\u53d1\u8005\u901a\u8fc7\u4e8b\u4ef6\u53d1\u5c04\u5b8c\u5168\u63a7\u5236\u6267\u884c\u6d41\u7a0b\u3002\n\n### @step\u88c5\u9970\u5668\u901a\u8fc7\u7c7b\u578b\u6ce8\u89e3\u5b9e\u73b0\u81ea\u52a8\u8def\u7531\n\n\u6e90\u7801\u4f4d\u4e8e`workflows/decorators.py`\uff0c\u88c5\u9970\u5668\u7b7e\u540d\uff1a\n\n```python\ndef step(\n    func: Callable | None = None,\n    *,\n    workflow: Type[\"Workflow\"] | None = None,\n    num_workers: int = 4,\n    retry_policy: RetryPolicy | None = None,\n) -> StepFunction\n```\n\n**\u6838\u5fc3\u529f\u80fd**\u5305\u62ec\uff1a\u7b7e\u540d\u68c0\u67e5\uff08\u63a8\u65ad\u8f93\u5165/\u8f93\u51fa\u4e8b\u4ef6\u7c7b\u578b\uff09\u3001Context\u53c2\u6570\u68c0\u6d4b\u3001\u8d44\u6e90\u6ce8\u5165\uff08\u901a\u8fc7`typing.Annotated`\uff09\u3001\u8fd0\u884c\u524d\u9a8c\u8bc1\u3002\u4e24\u79cd\u4f7f\u7528\u65b9\u5f0f\uff1a\n\n```python\n# \u7c7b\u65b9\u6cd5\u65b9\u5f0f - \u81ea\u52a8\u5173\u8054\u5230Workflow\nclass MyFlow(Workflow):\n    @step\n    async def start(self, ev: StartEvent) -> StopEvent:\n        return StopEvent(result=\"done\")\n\n# \u81ea\u7531\u51fd\u6570\u65b9\u5f0f - \u9700\u663e\u5f0f\u6307\u5b9aworkflow\n@step(workflow=MyWorkflow)\nasync def generate(ev: StartEvent) -> NextEvent: ...\n```\n\n**StartEvent**\u6807\u8bc6\u5165\u53e3\u70b9\uff0c\u53ef\u643a\u5e26\u4efb\u610f\u5c5e\u6027\uff1b**StopEvent**\u89e6\u53d1\u7acb\u5373\u505c\u6b62\u5e76\u901a\u8fc7`result`\u53c2\u6570\u8fd4\u56de\u503c\u3002\u81ea\u5b9a\u4e49\u4e8b\u4ef6\u57fa\u4e8ePydantic\u6a21\u578b\uff0c\u4e8b\u4ef6\u7c7b\u578b\u540c\u65f6\u5b9a\u4e49\u8def\u7531\u5173\u7cfb\u548c\u4f20\u9012\u6570\u636e\u3002\n\n### \u4e8b\u4ef6\u6d41\u901a\u8fc7asyncio\u961f\u5217\u5b9e\u73b0\u5e76\u53d1\u5206\u53d1\n\nWorkflow\u7c7b\u6838\u5fc3\u5b9e\u73b0\uff08\u6e90\u7801`workflows/workflow.py`\uff09\u57fa\u4e8easyncio\u961f\u5217\u67b6\u6784\uff1a\u6bcf\u4e2astep\u4f5c\u4e3aasync\u51fd\u6570\u4ece\u961f\u5217\u6d88\u8d39\u4e8b\u4ef6\uff0c\u53d1\u5c04\u7684\u65b0\u4e8b\u4ef6\u653e\u5165\u5176\u4ed6\u961f\u5217\u4f9b\u540e\u7eedstep\u5904\u7406\u3002\u8fd0\u884c\u6d41\u7a0b\uff1a`run()` \u2192 \u5173\u952e\u5b57\u53c2\u6570\u6253\u5305\u4e3aStartEvent \u2192 \u5206\u53d1\u5230\u8ba2\u9605steps \u2192 \u5904\u7406\u5e76\u53d1\u5c04\u65b0\u4e8b\u4ef6 \u2192 \u68c0\u6d4bStopEvent\u9000\u51fa\u3002\n\n**\u6761\u4ef6\u5206\u652f**\u901a\u8fc7`Context.send_event()`\u5b9e\u73b0\uff0c\u652f\u6301\u5411\u961f\u5217\u53d1\u9001\u591a\u4e2a\u4e8b\u4ef6\u89e6\u53d1\u5e76\u884c\u5206\u652f\u3002**\u5faa\u73af\u63a7\u5236**\u901a\u8fc7\u4e8b\u4ef6\u7c7b\u578b\u7684Union\u5b9e\u73b0\uff1a\n\n```python\n@step\nasync def extract(self, ev: StartEvent | ValidationErrorEvent) -> StopEvent | ExtractionDone:\n    if isinstance(ev, ValidationErrorEvent):\n        # \u5904\u7406\u9a8c\u8bc1\u9519\u8bef\uff0c\u53ef\u80fd\u89e6\u53d1\u91cd\u8bd5\u5faa\u73af\n```\n\n`collect_events()`\u65b9\u6cd5\u652f\u6301\u6536\u96c6\u6a21\u5f0f\uff0c\u7b49\u5f85\u6307\u5b9a\u6570\u91cf\u7684\u4e8b\u4ef6\u540e\u7ee7\u7eed\u6267\u884c\u3002\n\n### \u72b6\u6001\u6301\u4e45\u5316\u4e0eHuman-in-the-Loop\u96c6\u6210\n\n**Context\u5bf9\u8c61**\u652f\u6301\u7c7b\u578b\u5316\u72b6\u6001\u6a21\u578b\uff1a\n\n```python\nclass RunState(BaseModel):\n    num_runs: int = Field(default=0)\n\n@step\nasync def start(self, ctx: Context[RunState], ev: StartEvent) -> MyEvent:\n    async with ctx.store.edit_state() as state:\n        state.num_runs += 1\n```\n\n**WorkflowCheckpointer**\u63d0\u4f9b\u68c0\u67e5\u70b9\u673a\u5236\uff0c\u8bb0\u5f55`ctx_state`\uff08\u5b8c\u6574\u72b6\u6001\u5feb\u7167\uff09\u3001`queues`\uff08\u4e8b\u4ef6\u961f\u5217\uff09\u3001`broker_log`\uff08\u5df2\u5904\u7406\u4e8b\u4ef6\uff09\u3001`in_progress`\uff08\u8fdb\u884c\u4e2dstep\uff09\u3002\u901a\u8fc7`workflow.run_from(checkpoint)`\u53ef\u4ece\u4efb\u610f\u68c0\u67e5\u70b9\u6062\u590d\u6267\u884c\u3002\n\n**HITL\u96c6\u6210**\u901a\u8fc7\u5185\u7f6e\u4e8b\u4ef6\u7c7b\u578b\u5b9e\u73b0\uff1a\n\n```python\nresponse = await ctx.wait_for_event(\n    waiter_event=InputRequiredEvent(prefix=\"Confirm?\"),\n    event_type=HumanResponseEvent\n)\n```\n\nContext\u652f\u6301\u5e8f\u5217\u5316\uff08`JsonSerializer`/`JsonPickleSerializer`\uff09\uff0c\u9002\u7528\u4e8e\u957f\u65f6\u95f4\u7b49\u5f85\u573a\u666f\u4e0b\u7684\u72b6\u6001\u6301\u4e45\u5316\u3002\n\n### \u7b2c57\u7ae0\uff1aAgentWorkflow\u4e0e\u5de5\u5177\u96c6\u6210\n\n### AgentWorkflow\u6784\u5efa\u4e862025\u5e74Agent-First\u67b6\u6784\u7684\u6838\u5fc3\n\n\u6e90\u7801\u8def\u5f84`llama_index/core/agent/workflow/`\uff0cAgent\u7c7b\u5c42\u6b21\u7ed3\u6784\uff1a\n\n```\nBaseWorkflowAgent (\u57fa\u7c7b)\n    \u251c\u2500\u2500 FunctionAgent (\u51fd\u6570\u8c03\u7528Agent - OpenAI Function Calling)\n    \u251c\u2500\u2500 ReActAgent (ReAct\u63a8\u7406Agent - \u601d\u8003-\u884c\u52a8\u5faa\u73af)\n    \u2514\u2500\u2500 CodeActAgent (\u4ee3\u7801\u6267\u884cAgent - Python\u4ee3\u7801\u751f\u6210)\n```\n\n**AgentWorkflow vs \u6807\u51c6Workflow**\u7684\u6838\u5fc3\u5dee\u5f02\uff1a\n\n| \u7279\u6027 | \u6807\u51c6Workflow | AgentWorkflow |\n|------|-------------|---------------|\n| \u62bd\u8c61\u7ea7\u522b | \u4f4e\u7ea7\u4e8b\u4ef6\u9a71\u52a8 | \u9ad8\u7ea7Agent\u7f16\u6392 |\n| LLM\u96c6\u6210 | \u624b\u52a8\u5b9e\u73b0 | \u5185\u7f6e\u5de5\u5177\u8c03\u7528/\u63a8\u7406 |\n| \u72b6\u6001\u7ba1\u7406 | \u624b\u52a8Context | \u81ea\u52a8state/memory |\n| \u591aAgent | \u9700\u81ea\u5b9a\u4e49 | \u5185\u7f6ehandoff\u673a\u5236 |\n\nAgent\u5faa\u73af\u5b9e\u73b0\u9075\u5faa\u56fa\u5b9a\u6d41\u7a0b\uff1a`init_run()` \u2192 `setup_agent()` \u2192 `run_agent_step()` \u2192 `parse_agent_output()` \u2192 \u6709tool_calls\u5219`call_tool()` \u2192 \u65e0\u5219\u8fd4\u56deStopEvent\u3002\n\n### FunctionTool\u901a\u8fc7\u7c7b\u578b\u63a8\u65ad\u5b9e\u73b0\u96f6\u914d\u7f6e\u5de5\u5177\u5c01\u88c5\n\n\u6e90\u7801\u8def\u5f84`llama_index/core/tools/function_tool.py`\uff0c**\u81ea\u52a8\u7c7b\u578b\u63a8\u65ad\u673a\u5236**\uff1a\n\n- \u51fd\u6570\u540d \u2192 \u5de5\u5177\u540d\u79f0\n- Docstring \u2192 \u5de5\u5177\u63cf\u8ff0\n- \u7c7b\u578b\u6ce8\u89e3 \u2192 \u53c2\u6570Schema\n- `Annotated[type, \"description\"]` \u2192 \u53c2\u6570\u63cf\u8ff0\n\n```python\ndef wolfram_alpha_query(self, query: str):\n    \"\"\"Make a query to wolfram alpha about a mathematical problem.\n    Args:\n        query (str): The query to be passed to wolfram alpha.\n    \"\"\"\n# \u81ea\u52a8\u8f6c\u6362\u4e3aOpenAI Function\u683c\u5f0f\n```\n\n**ToolSpec**\u63d0\u4f9b\u5de5\u5177\u7ec4\u5c01\u88c5\uff0c`spec_functions`\u5217\u8868\u5b9a\u4e49\u8981\u5bfc\u51fa\u7684\u51fd\u6570\uff0c`to_tool_list()`\u65b9\u6cd5\u6279\u91cf\u8f6c\u6362\u3002**\u53c2\u6570\u9a8c\u8bc1**\u5728`BaseWorkflowAgent.validate_tools()`\u4e2d\u81ea\u52a8\u5c06\u975eBaseTool\u5bf9\u8c61\u8f6c\u6362\u4e3aFunctionTool\uff0c\u5e76\u7981\u6b62\u4f7f\u7528\u4fdd\u7559\u540d\u79f0\"handoff\"\u3002\n\n### \u591a\u667a\u80fd\u4f53\u7f16\u6392\u901a\u8fc7handoff\u673a\u5236\u5b9e\u73b0Agent\u5207\u6362\n\n```python\nagent_workflow = AgentWorkflow(\n    agents=[research_agent, write_agent, BaseRetriever\\u63a5\\u53e3\\u8bbe\\u8ba1\\u5b9e\\u73b0\\u4e86\\u68c0\\u7d22\\u7b56\\u7565\\u7684\\u7075\\u6d3b\\u7ec4\\u5408\\n\\n\\u6e90\\u7801\\u8def\\u5f84`llama-index-core/llama_index/core/base/base_retriever.py`\\uff0c\\u6838\\u5fc3\\u65b9\\u6cd5\\u7b7e\\u540d\\uff1a\\n\\n```python\\nclass **AutoMergingRetriever**\\uff1a\\u68c0\\u7d22\\u53f6\\u5b50\\u8282\\u70b9\\u540e\\u81ea\\u52a8\\u5408\\u5e76\\u4e3a\\u7236\\u8282\\u70b9\\u4e0a\\u4e0b\\u6587\\n5. **QueryFusionRetriever**\\uff1a\\u591a\\u68c0\\u7d22\\u5668\\u7ed3\\u679c\\u878d\\u5408\\uff0c\\u652f\\u6301\\u67e5\\u8be2\\u53d8\\u4f53\\u751f\\u6210\\n\\n### \\u6df7\\u5408\\u68c0\\u7d22\\u901a\\u8fc7\\u5012\\u6570\\u6392\\u540d\\u878d\\u5408\\u5b9e\\u73b0\\u6700\\u4f73\\u53ec\\u56de\\n\\n\\u6e90\\u7801\\u8def\\u5f84`llama-index-core/llama_index/core/retrievers/fusion_retriever.py`\\uff0c\\u6838\\u5fc3\\u7b97\\u6cd5\\u4e3a**Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF)**\\uff1a\\n\\n```python\\ndef reciprocal_rank_fusion(results_list, k=60):\\n    fused_scores = {}\\n    for results in results_list:\\n        for rank, node in enumerate(results):\\n            fused_scores[node.id] = fused_scores.get(node.id, 0) + 1.0 / (rank + k)\\n    return sorted(fused_scores.items(), key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\\n```\\n\\n\\u53c2\\u6570`k`\\u63a7\\u5236\\u5f02\\u5e38\\u6392\\u540d\\u7684\\u5f71\\u54cd\\uff0c\\u9ed8\\u8ba460\\u3002\\u878d\\u5408\\u6a21\\u5f0f\\u652f\\u6301`reciprocal_rerank`\\uff08RRF\\uff09\\u3001`simple`\\uff08\\u7b80\\u5355\\u5408\\u5e76\\uff09\\u3001`dist_based`\\uff08\\u57fa\\u4e8e\\u5206\\u5e03\\uff09\\u3002\\u5178\\u578b\\u914d\\u7f6e\\u4e3aBM25+Vector\\u6df7\\u5408\\uff1a\\u521d\\u59cb\\u68c0\\u7d22`similarity_top_k=10-20`\\uff0c\\u901a\\u8fc7\\u91cd\\u6392\\u5e8f\\u5668\\u7b5b\\u9009\\u5230\\u6700\\u7ec82-5\\u4e2a\\u7ed3\\u679c\\u3002\\n\\n### pie... It is generally double-crusted...\nAnswer: YES\n\nInformation: Apple pies tastes bad.\nContext: An apple pie is a fruit pie...\nAnswer: NO\n\nInformation: {query_str}\nContext: \u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u81ea\u5b9a\u4e49\u673a\u5236\n\n`PromptTemplate`\u57fa\u7c7b\u5b9a\u4e49\u4e86\u6838\u5fc3\u6269\u5c55\u63a5\u53e3\u3002\u5173\u952e\u5c5e\u6027\u5305\u62ec`template_vars`\uff08\u6a21\u677f\u53d8\u91cf\u5217\u8868\uff09\u3001`output_parser`\uff08\u8f93\u51fa\u89e3\u6790\u5668\uff09\u3001`template_var_mappings`\uff08\u53d8\u91cf\u91cd\u6620\u5c04\uff09\u548c`function_mappings`\uff08\u52a8\u6001\u51fd\u6570\u6ce8\u5165\uff09\u3002\n\n**PromptSelector**\u673a\u5236\u5b9e\u73b0\u4e86\u6a21\u578b\u611f\u77e5\u7684\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u9009\u62e9\u3002`SelectorPromptTemplate`\u6839\u636eLLM\u7c7b\u578b\uff08chat vs completion\uff09\u81ea\u52a8\u5207\u6362\u5bf9\u5e94\u683c\u5f0f\uff1a\n\n```python\nSelectorPromptTemplate(\n    default_template=PromptTemplate(...),\n    conditionals=[\n        (is_chat_model, \u7b2c59\u7ae0\uff1aLangChain\u4e0eLangGraph\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u4f53\u7cfb\n\nLangChain\u7684\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u8bbe\u8ba1\u9075\u5faa**\u6a21\u5757\u5316\u4e0e\u53ef\u7ec4\u5408\u6027**\u539f\u5219\uff0c\u901a\u8fc7Hub\u751f\u6001\u5b9e\u73b0\u7248\u672c\u7ba1\u7406\u548c\u793e\u533a\u5171\u4eab\u3002LangGraph\u5219\u4ee3\u8868\u4e86\u8bbe\u8ba1\u8303\u5f0f\u7684\u8f6c\u53d8\u2014\u2014**\u4ece\u9690\u5f0f\u9ed8\u8ba4\u5230\u663e\u5f0f\u5b9a\u4e49**\u3002\n\n### ReAct Agent\u7684\u7ecf\u5178\u63a8\u7406\u5faa\u73af\n\nReAct Agent\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\uff08`hwchase17/react`\uff09\u662fLangChain\u6700\u5177\u5f71\u54cd\u529b\u7684\u6a21\u677f\u4e4b\u4e00\uff0c\u5b9a\u4e49\u4e86\"\u601d\u8003-\u884c\u52a8-\u89c2\u5bdf\"\u7684\u63a8\u7406\u8303\u5f0f\uff1a\n\n```\nAnswer the following questions as best you can. You have access to the following tools:\n\n{tools}\n\nUse the following format:\n\nQuestion: the input question you must answer\nThought: you should always think about what to do\nAction: the action to take, should be one of [{tool_names}]\nAction Input: the input to the action\nObservation: the result of the action\n... (this Thought/Action/Action Input/Observation can repeat N times)\nThought: I now know the final answer\nFinal Answer: the final answer to the original input question\n\nBegin!\n\nQuestion: {input}\nThought:{agent_scratchpad}\n```\n\n\u56db\u4e2a\u5173\u952e\u53d8\u91cf\u9a71\u52a8\u6574\u4e2a\u5faa\u73af\uff1a`{tools}`\u63d0\u4f9b\u5de5\u5177\u63cf\u8ff0\uff0c`{tool_names}`\u7ea6\u675f\u884c\u52a8\u7a7a\u95f4\uff0c`{input}`\u63a5\u6536\u7528\u6237\u95ee\u9898\uff0c`{agent_scratchpad}`\u7d2f\u79ef\u4e2d\u95f4\u63a8\u7406\u6b65\u9aa4\u3002LangGraph\u7684`create_react_agent`\u5219**\u5b8c\u5168\u653e\u5f03\u9ed8\u8ba4\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd**\uff0c\u8981\u6c42\u7528\u6237\u901a\u8fc7`prompt`\u53c2\u6570\u663e\u5f0f\u63d0\u4f9b\u3002\n\n### Router\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u7684\u5206\u7c7b\u51b3\u7b56\n\n**MULTI_PROMPT_ROUTER_TEMPLATE**\u5b9e\u73b0\u4e86\u57fa\u4e8e\u8bed\u4e49\u7684\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u8def\u7531\uff1a\n\n```\nGiven a raw text input to a language model select the model prompt best suited \nfor the input. You will be given the names of the available prompts and a \ndescription of what the prompt is best suited for.\n\n<< CANDIDATE PROMPTS >>\n{destinations}\n\n<< INPUT >>\n{input}\n\n<< OUTPUT >>\n```\n\n\u73b0\u4ee3LangGraph\u5b9e\u8df5\u503e\u5411\u4e8e\u4f7f\u7528\u7ed3\u6784\u5316\u8f93\u51fa\u66ff\u4ee3\u5b57\u7b26\u4e32\u89e3\u6790\uff1a\n\n```python\nclass RouteQuery(TypedDict):\n    \"\"\"Route query to destination.\"\"\"\n    destination: Literal[\"animal\", \"vegetable\"]\n\nroute_chain = route_prompt | llm.with_structured_output(RouteQuery)\n```\n\n### RAG\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u7684\u56db\u79cd\u7ec4\u5408\u6a21\u5f0f\n\nLangChain\u63d0\u4f9b\u4e86\u56db\u79cd\u6587\u6863\u7ec4\u5408\u7b56\u7565\uff0c\u6bcf\u79cd\u5bf9\u5e94\u4e0d\u540c\u7684\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u6a21\u677f\uff1a\n\n**Stuff\u6a21\u5f0f**\u6700\u7b80\u5355\u76f4\u63a5\uff0c\u5c06\u6240\u6709\u4e0a\u4e0b\u6587\u62fc\u63a5\u540e\u4e00\u6b21\u6027\u5904\u7406\uff1a\n```\nUse \u7b2c60\u7ae0\uff1aGraphRAG\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u9006\u5411\u5de5\u7a0b\n\n\u5fae\u8f6fGraphRAG\u7684\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u8bbe\u8ba1\u4f53\u73b0\u4e86**\u7ed3\u6784\u5316\u4fe1\u606f\u62bd\u53d6**\u548c**\u591a\u9636\u6bb5\u63a8\u7406**\u7684\u72ec\u7279\u65b9\u6cd5\u8bba\u3002\u4e0eLlamaIndex\u548cLangChain\u7684\u95ee\u7b54\u5bfc\u5411\u4e0d\u540c\uff0cGraphRAG\u7684\u6838\u5fc3\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u4e13\u6ce8\u4e8e\u77e5\u8bc6\u56fe\u8c31\u7684\u6784\u5efa\u4e0e\u67e5\u8be2\u3002\n\n### \u5b9e\u4f53\u62bd\u53d6\u7684\u7ed3\u6784\u5316\u683c\u5f0f\n\n**entity_extraction\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd**\uff08\u4f4d\u4e8e`graphrag/prompts/index/extract_graph.py`\uff09\u5b9a\u4e49\u4e86\u4e25\u683c\u7684\u8f93\u51fa\u683c\u5f0f\u89c4\u8303\uff1a\n\n```\n-Goal-\nGiven a text document that is potentially relevant to this activity and a list of \nentity types, identify all entities of those types from the text and all relationships \namong the identified entities.\n\n-Steps-\n1. Identify all entities. For each identified entity, extract the following information:\n- entity_name: Name of the entity, capitalized\n- entity_type: One of the following types: [{entity_types}]\n- entity_description: Comprehensive description of the entity's attributes and activities\nFormat each entity as (\"entity\"{tuple_delimiter}<entity_name>{tuple_delimiter}<entity_type>{tuple_delimiter}<entity_description>)\n\n2. From the entities identified in step 1, identify all pairs of (source_entity, target_entity) \nthat are *clearly related* to each other.\n...\nFormat each relationship as (\"relationship\"{tuple_delimiter}<source_entity>{tuple_delimiter}<target_entity>{tuple_delimiter}<relationship_description>{tuple_delimiter}<relationship_strength>)\n```\n\n**\u5173\u952e\u8bbe\u8ba1\u7279\u5f81**\u5305\u62ec\uff1a\u81ea\u5b9a\u4e49\u5206\u9694\u7b26\u7cfb\u7edf\uff08`{tuple_delimiter}`\u3001`{record_delimiter}`\uff09\u786e\u4fdd\u8f93\u51fa\u53ef\u9760\u89e3\u6790\uff1b\u5173\u7cfb\u5f3a\u5ea6\u4ee5\u6570\u503c\u5206\u6570\uff081-10\uff09\u8868\u793a\uff0c\u652f\u6301\u56fe\u4e0a\u7684\u52a0\u6743\u5206\u6790\uff1bFew-shot\u793a\u4f8b\u4f7f\u7528\u865a\u6784\u7684\"Verdantis\"\u573a\u666f\uff0c\u907f\u514d\u4e0e\u771f\u5b9e\u5b9e\u4f53\u6df7\u6dc6\u3002\n\n**\u5faa\u73af\u62bd\u53d6\u673a\u5236\uff08Gleaning\uff09**\u901a\u8fc7\u591a\u8f6e\u63d0\u793a\u786e\u4fdd\u62bd\u53d6\u5b8c\u6574\u6027\uff1a\n```\nCONTINUE_PROMPT = \"MANY entities and relationships were missed in the last extraction. \nRemember to ONLY emit entities that match any of the previously extracted types. \nAdd them below using the same format:\\n\"\n\nLOOP_PROMPT = \"It appears some entities and relationships may have still been missed. \nAnswer YES | NO if there are still entities that need to be added.\\n\"\n```\n\n### \u58f0\u660e\u62bd\u53d6\u7684\u65f6\u95f4\u4e0e\u7f6e\u4fe1\u5ea6\u5efa\u6a21\n\n**claim_extraction\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd**\u6269\u5c55\u4e86\u5b9e\u4f53\u62bd\u53d6\u8303\u5f0f\uff0c\u589e\u52a0\u4e86\u65f6\u95f4\u7ef4\u5ea6\u548c\u7f6e\u4fe1\u5ea6\u5224\u65ad\uff1a\n\n```\nFor each claim, extract the following information:\n- Subject: name of the entity that is subject of the claim\n- Object: name of the entity that is object of the claim\n- Claim Type: overall category of the claim\n- Claim Status: TRUE, FALSE, or SUSPECTED\n- Claim Description: Detailed description explaining the reasoning\n- Claim Date: Period (start_date, end_date) when the claim was made (ISO-8601 format)\n- Claim Source Text: List of all quotes from the original text relevant to the claim\n```\n\n\u4e09\u5143\u7ec4\u72b6\u6001\u8bbe\u8ba1\uff08TRUE/FALSE/SUSPECTED\uff09\u652f\u6301\u4e0d\u786e\u5b9a\u6027\u5efa\u6a21\uff0c\u8fd9\u5728\u6cd5\u5f8b\u3001\u91d1\u878d\u7b49\u9700\u8981\u533a\u5206\u5df2\u786e\u8ba4\u4e8b\u5b9e\u4e0e\u7591\u4f3c\u4fe1\u606f\u7684\u9886\u57df\u5c24\u4e3a\u91cd\u8981\u3002\n\n### \u793e\u533a\u6458\u8981\u7684\u5c42\u7ea7\u7b56\u7565\n\n**summarize_descriptions\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd**\u5904\u7406\u540c\u4e00\u5b9e\u4f53\u7684\u591a\u4e2a\u63cf\u8ff0\u7247\u6bb5\uff0c\u8981\u6c42\u6a21\u578b\u89e3\u51b3\u6f5c\u5728\u77db\u76fe\u5e76\u751f\u6210\u7edf\u4e00\u6458\u8981\uff1a\n\n```\nYou are a helpful assistant responsible for generating a comprehensive summary \nof the data provided below.\nGiven one or more entities, and a list of descriptions, all related to the same \nentity or group of entities.\nPlease concatenate all of these into a single, comprehensive description. \nMake sure to include information collected from all the descriptions.\nIf the provided descriptions are contradictory, please resolve the contradictions \nand provide a single, coherent summary.\n```\n\n**\u793e\u533a\u62a5\u544a\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd**\u8981\u6c42\u751f\u6210\u7ed3\u6784\u5316\u7684JSON\u8f93\u51fa\uff0c\u5305\u542b\u6807\u9898\u3001\u6458\u8981\u3001\u5f71\u54cd\u4e25\u91cd\u6027\u8bc4\u5206\uff080-10\uff09\u548c\u8be6\u7ec6\u53d1\u73b0\u5217\u8868\u3002**Grounding Rules**\u662fGraphRAG\u7684\u72ec\u7279\u8bbe\u8ba1\uff0c\u5f3a\u5236\u6a21\u578b\u5f15\u7528\u6570\u636e\u6765\u6e90\uff1a\n\n```\nPoints supported by data should list their data references as follows:\n\"This is an example sentence supported by multiple data references \n[Data: <dataset name> (record ids); <dataset name> (record ids)].\"\n\nDo not list more than 5 record ids in a single reference. Instead, list the top 5 \nmost relevant record ids and add \"+more\" to indicate that there are more.\n```\n\n### \u67e5\u8be2\u9636\u6bb5\u7684Local/Global/DRIFT\u4e09\u6a21\u5f0f\n\n**Local Search\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd**\u9762\u5411\u7cbe\u786e\u7684\u5b9e\u4f53\u7ea7\u67e5\u8be2\uff0c\u76f4\u63a5\u4f7f\u7528\u7d22\u5f15\u6570\u636e\u8868\uff1a\n\n```\n---Role---\nYou are a helpful assistant responding to questions about data in the tables provided.\n\n---Goal---\nGenerate a response of the target length and format that responds to the user's question, \nsummarizing all information in the input data tables appropriate for the response length \nand format, and incorporating any relevant general knowledge.\n```\n\n**Global Search\u91c7\u7528Map-Reduce\u67b6\u6784**\u3002Map\u9636\u6bb5\u4ece\u5404\u793e\u533a\u62a5\u544a\u4e2d\u63d0\u53d6\u5173\u952e\u70b9\u5e76\u8bc4\u5206\uff080-100\uff09\uff0cReduce\u9636\u6bb5\u6309\u91cd\u8981\u6027\u964d\u5e8f\u5408\u5e76\u8fd9\u4e9b\u5206\u6790\u5e08\u62a5\u544a\u3002\u8fd9\u79cd\u8bbe\u8ba1\u4f7fGraphRAG\u80fd\u591f\u56de\u7b54\u9700\u8981\u7efc\u5408\u591a\u4e2a\u793e\u533a\u77e5\u8bc6\u7684\u5b8f\u89c2\u95ee\u9898\u3002\n\n**DRIFT Search**\uff08Dynamic Reasoning and Inference with Flexible Traversal\uff09\u662f2024\u5e74\u5f15\u5165\u7684\u65b0\u6a21\u5f0f\uff0c\u7ed3\u5408\u4e86Local\u548cGlobal\u641c\u7d22\u7684\u4f18\u52bf\u3002\u5176Primer\u9636\u6bb5\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u8981\u6c42\u6a21\u578b\u63d0\u4f9b\u4e2d\u95f4\u7b54\u6848\u3001\u76f8\u5173\u6027\u8bc4\u5206\u548c\u540e\u7eed\u67e5\u8be2\u5efa\u8bae\uff1a\n\n```\nYou will begin your reasoning looking at a summary of the content of the most \nrelevant communities and will provide:\n\n1. score: How well the intermediate answer addresses the query (0-100)\n2. intermediate_answer: This answer should match the level of detail and length \n   found in the community summaries. Exactly 2000 characters long.\n3. follow_up_queries: A list of follow-up queries that could be asked to further \n   explore the topic.\n```\n\n### \u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u81ea\u52a8\u8c03\u4f18\u5b9e\u9a8c\n\nGraphRAG\u63d0\u4f9b\u4e86CLI\u7ea7\u522b\u7684\u63d0\u793a\u8bcd\u81ea\u52a8\u8c03\u4f18\u529f\u80fd\uff1a\n\n```bash\ngraphrag prompt-tune Answer:\\n```\\n\\nChat\\u7248\\u672c\\uff08`CHAT_REFINE_PROMPT`\\uff09\\u66f4\\u8fdb\\u4e00\\u6b65\\uff0c\\u5c06\\u884c\\u4e3a\\u6a21\\u5f0f\\u7b80\\u5316\\u4e3a\\u4e8c\\u5143\\u9009\\u62e9\\uff1a\\\"**Rewrite**\\u4f7f\\u7528\\u65b0\\u4e0a\\u4e0b\\u6587\\u91cd\\u5199\\uff0c\\u6216**Repeat**\\u4fdd\\u7559\\u539f\\u7b54\\u6848\\\"\\u3002\\u8fd9\\u79cd\\u8bbe\\u8ba1\\u6709\\u6548\\u964d\\u4f4e\\u4e86\\u6a21\\u578b\\u5728\\u8fed\\u4ee3\\u8fc7\\u7a0b\\u4e2d\\u5f15\\u5165\\u5e7b\\u89c9\\u7684\\u98ce\\u9669\\u3002\\n\\n### \\u95ee\\u7b54\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u7684\\u89d2\\u8272\\u7ea6\\u675f\\n\\nLlamaIndex\\u7684QA\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u5728Chat\\u683c\\u5f0f\\u4e2d\\u5f15\\u5165\\u4e86\\u663e\\u5f0f\\u7684\\u89d2\\u8272\\u7ea6\\u675f\\u89c4\\u5219\\u3002\\u7cfb\\u7edf\\u6d88\\u606f\\u5b9a\\u4e49\\u4e86\\u4e00\\u4e2a\\\"\\u5168\\u7403\\u53d7\\u4fe1\\u4efb\\u7684\\u4e13\\u5bb6\\u95ee\\u7b54\\u7cfb\\u7edf\\\"\\u89d2\\u8272\\uff0c\\u5e76\\u9644\\u5e26\\u660e\\u786e\\u7684\\u884c\\u4e3a\\u7981\\u4ee4\\uff1a\\n\\n```python\\nChatMessage(\\n    role=MessageRole.SYSTEM,\\n    content=(\\n        \\\"You are an expert Q&A system that is trusted around the world.\\\\n\\\"\\n        \\\"Always answer the query using the provided context ),\\n)\\n```\\n\\n**CondenseQuestion\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd**\\uff08\\u4f4d\\u4e8e`llama_index/core/chat_engine/condense_question.py`\\uff09\\u89e3\\u51b3\\u4e86\\u591a\\u8f6e\\u5bf9\\u8bdd\\u4e2d\\u7684\\u6307\\u4ee3\\u6d88\\u89e3\\u95ee\\u9898\\u3002\\u5b83\\u5c06\\u5bf9\\u8bdd\\u5386\\u53f2\\u548c\\u540e\\u7eed\\u95ee\\u9898\\u538b\\u7f29\\u4e3a\\u72ec\\u7acb\\u7684\\u67e5\\u8be2\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\nGiven a conversation (between Human and Assistant) and a follow up message from Human,\\nrewrite the message to be a standalone question that captures all relevant context \\nfrom the \\u7ed3\\u6784\\u5316\\u8f93\\u51fa\\u7684Schema\\u9a71\\u52a8\\u6a21\\u5f0f\\n\\nLlamaIndex\\u5bf9\\u7ed3\\u6784\\u5316\\u8f93\\u51fa\\u91c7\\u7528**Schema\\u9a71\\u52a8\\u751f\\u6210**\\u6a21\\u5f0f\\u3002`OpenAIPydanticProgram`\\u5229\\u7528\\u51fd\\u6570\\u8c03\\u7528\\u80fd\\u529b\\u76f4\\u63a5\\u7ed1\\u5b9aPydantic\\u6a21\\u578b\\uff0c\\u800c\\u975e\\u51fd\\u6570\\u8c03\\u7528LLM\\u5219\\u9700\\u8981\\u663e\\u5f0f\\u6ce8\\u5165JSON chat role=\\\"system\\\" %}`\\u5757\\u53ef\\u4ee5\\u76f4\\u63a5\\u5b9a\\u4e49\\u6d88\\u606f\\u89d2\\u8272\\uff0c`{{ variable | image \\u8bc4\\u4f30\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u7684Few-shot\\u8bbe\\u8ba1\\n\\n**Faithfulness\\u8bc4\\u4f30\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd**\\u5c55\\u793a\\u4e86LlamaIndex\\u5bf9Few-shot\\u5b66\\u4e60\\u7684\\u5178\\u578b\\u5e94\\u7528\\u3002\\u5b83\\u63d0\\u4f9b\\u4e86\\u6b63\\u53cd\\u4e24\\u4e2a\\u793a\\u4f8b\\u6765\\u6821\\u51c6\\u6a21\\u578b\\u7684\\u5224\\u65ad\\u6807\\u51c6\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\nPlease \\u7b2c59\\u7ae0\\uff1aLangChain\\u4e0eLangGraph\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u4f53\\u7cfb\\n\\nLangChain\\u7684\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u8bbe\\u8ba1\\u9075\\u5faa**\\u6a21\\u5757\\u5316\\u4e0e\\u53ef\\u7ec4\\u5408\\u6027**\\u539f\\u5219\\uff0c\\u901a\\u8fc7Hub\\u751f\\u6001\\u5b9e\\u73b0\\u7248\\u672c\\u7ba1\\u7406\\u548c\\u793e\\u533a\\u5171\\u4eab\\u3002LangGraph\\u5219\\u4ee3\\u8868\\u4e86\\u8bbe\\u8ba1\\u8303\\u5f0f\\u7684\\u8f6c\\u53d8\\u2014\\u2014**\\u4ece\\u9690\\u5f0f\\u9ed8\\u8ba4\\u5230\\u663e\\u5f0f\\u5b9a\\u4e49**\\u3002\\n\\n### ReAct Agent\\u7684\\u7ecf\\u5178\\u63a8\\u7406\\u5faa\\u73af\\n\\nReAct You have access to the following tools:\\n\\n{tools}\\n\\nUse the following format:\\n\\nQuestion: the input question you must answer\\nThought: you should always think about what to do\\nAction: the action to take, should be one of [{tool_names}]\\nAction Input: the input to the action\\nObservation: the result of the action\\n... (this Thought/Action/Action Input/Observation can repeat N times)\\nThought: I now know the final answer\\nFinal Answer: the final answer to the original input question\\n\\nBegin!\\n\\nQuestion: {input}\\nThought:{agent_scratchpad}\\n```\\n\\n\\u56db\\u4e2a\\u5173\\u952e\\u53d8\\u91cf\\u9a71\\u52a8\\u6574\\u4e2a\\u5faa\\u73af\\uff1a`{tools}`\\u63d0\\u4f9b\\u5de5\\u5177\\u63cf\\u8ff0\\uff0c`{tool_names}`\\u7ea6\\u675f\\u884c\\u52a8\\u7a7a\\u95f4\\uff0c`{input}`\\u63a5\\u6536\\u7528\\u6237\\u95ee\\u9898\\uff0c`{agent_scratchpad}`\\u7d2f\\u79ef\\u4e2d\\u95f4\\u63a8\\u7406\\u6b65\\u9aa4\\u3002LangGraph\\u7684`create_react_agent`\\u5219**\\u5b8c\\u5168\\u653e\\u5f03\\u9ed8\\u8ba4\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd**\\uff0c\\u8981\\u6c42\\u7528\\u6237\\u901a\\u8fc7`prompt`\\u53c2\\u6570\\u663e\\u5f0f\\u63d0\\u4f9b\\u3002\\n\\n### Router\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u7684\\u5206\\u7c7b\\u51b3\\u7b56\\n\\n**MULTI_PROMPT_ROUTER_TEMPLATE**\\u5b9e\\u73b0\\u4e86\\u57fa\\u4e8e\\u8bed\\u4e49\\u7684\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u8def\\u7531\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\nGiven a raw text input to a language model select the model prompt best suited \\nfor the input. You will be given the names of the available prompts and a \\ndescription of what the prompt is best suited for.\\n\\n<< CANDIDATE PROMPTS >>\\n{destinations}\\n\\n<< INPUT >>\\n{input}\\n\\n<< OUTPUT >>\\n```\\n\\n\\u73b0\\u4ee3LangGraph\\u5b9e\\u8df5\\u503e\\u5411\\u4e8e\\u4f7f\\u7528\\u7ed3\\u6784\\u5316\\u8f93\\u51fa\\u66ff\\u4ee3\\u5b57\\u7b26\\u4e32\\u89e3\\u6790\\uff1a\\n\\n```python\\nclass Memory\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u7684\\u5b9e\\u4f53\\u8ddf\\u8e2a\\n\\n**ConversationSummaryMemory**\\u4f7f\\u7528\\u6e10\\u8fdb\\u5f0f\\u6458\\u8981\\u7b56\\u7565\\uff0c\\u6bcf\\u6b21\\u53ea\\u5904\\u7406\\u65b0\\u589e\\u5bf9\\u8bdd\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\nProgressively RAG\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u7684\\u56db\\u79cd\\u7ec4\\u5408\\u6a21\\u5f0f\\n\\nLangChain\\u63d0\\u4f9b\\u4e86\\u56db\\u79cd\\u6587\\u6863\\u7ec4\\u5408\\u7b56\\u7565\\uff0c\\u6bcf\\u79cd\\u5bf9\\u5e94\\u4e0d\\u540c\\u7684\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u6a21\\u677f\\uff1a\\n\\n**Stuff\\u6a21\\u5f0f**\\u6700\\u7b80\\u5355\\u76f4\\u63a5\\uff0c\\u5c06\\u6240\\u6709\\u4e0a\\u4e0b\\u6587\\u62fc\\u63a5\\u540e\\u4e00\\u6b21\\u6027\\u5904\\u7406\\uff1a\\n```\\nUse {question}\\nHelpful addition to giving an answer, also return a score of how fully it answered \\nthe user's question...\\n\\nHow to determine the score:\\n- Higher is a better answer\\n- If you do not know the answer based on the context, that should be a score of 0\\n- Don't be overconfident!\\n```\\n\\n### Hub\\u751f\\u6001\\u7684\\u6807\\u51c6\\u5316\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\n\\n**`rlm/rag-prompt`**\\u662fHub\\u4e0a\\u4f7f\\u7528\\u6700\\u5e7f\\u6cdb\\u7684RAG\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\uff0c\\u9488\\u5bf9\\u7b80\\u6d01\\u6027\\u4f18\\u5316\\uff1a\\n```\\nYou \\nAnswer:\\n```\\n\\nHub\\u652f\\u6301\\u7248\\u672c\\u9501\\u5b9a\\uff08`hub.pull(\\\"rlm/rag-prompt:50442af1\\\")`\\uff09\\u548c\\u6a21\\u578b\\u7279\\u5b9a\\u53d8\\u4f53\\uff08`rlm/rag-prompt-llama3`\\u3001`rlm/rag-prompt-mistral`\\uff09\\u3002\\n\\n### \\u7ed3\\u6784\\u5316\\u8f93\\u51fa\\u7684\\u6f14\\u8fdb\\n\\nLangChain\\u7684\\u7ed3\\u6784\\u5316\\u8f93\\u51fa\\u7ecf\\u5386\\u4e86\\u4ece`OutputParser`\\u5230`with_structured_output`\\u7684\\u6f14\\u8fdb\\u3002\\u65e7\\u6a21\\u5f0f\\u9700\\u8981\\u624b\\u52a8\\u6ce8\\u5165\\u683c\\u5f0f\\u6307\\u4ee4\\uff1a\\n\\n```python\\nformat_instructions \\u7b2c60\\u7ae0\\uff1aGraphRAG\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u9006\\u5411\\u5de5\\u7a0b\\n\\n\\u5fae\\u8f6fGraphRAG\\u7684\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u8bbe\\u8ba1\\u4f53\\u73b0\\u4e86**\\u7ed3\\u6784\\u5316\\u4fe1\\u606f\\u62bd\\u53d6**\\u548c**\\u591a\\u9636\\u6bb5\\u63a8\\u7406**\\u7684\\u72ec\\u7279\\u65b9\\u6cd5\\u8bba\\u3002\\u4e0eLlamaIndex\\u548cLangChain\\u7684\\u95ee\\u7b54\\u5bfc\\u5411\\u4e0d\\u540c\\uff0cGraphRAG\\u7684\\u6838\\u5fc3\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u4e13\\u6ce8\\u4e8e\\u77e5\\u8bc6\\u56fe\\u8c31\\u7684\\u6784\\u5efa\\u4e0e\\u67e5\\u8be2\\u3002\\n\\n### \\u5b9e\\u4f53\\u62bd\\u53d6\\u7684\\u7ed3\\u6784\\u5316\\u683c\\u5f0f\\n\\n**entity_extraction\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd**\\uff08\\u4f4d\\u4e8e`graphrag/prompts/index/extract_graph.py`\\uff09\\u5b9a\\u4e49\\u4e86\\u4e25\\u683c\\u7684\\u8f93\\u51fa\\u683c\\u5f0f\\u89c4\\u8303\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\n-Goal-\\nGiven a text document that is potentially relevant to this activity and a list of \\nentity types, identify all entities of those types from the text and all relationships \\namong the identified entities.\\n\\n-Steps-\\n1. Identify all entities. For each identified entity, extract the following information:\\n- entity_name: Name of the entity, capitalized\\n- entity_type: One of the following types: [{entity_types}]\\n- entity_description: Comprehensive description of the entity's attributes and activities\\nFormat each entity as (\\\"entity\\\"{tuple_delimiter}<entity_name>{tuple_delimiter}<entity_type>{tuple_delimiter}<entity_description>)\\n\\n2. From the entities identified in step 1, identify all pairs of (source_entity, target_entity) \\nthat are *clearly related* to each other.\\n...\\nFormat each relationship as (\\\"relationship\\\"{tuple_delimiter}<source_entity>{tuple_delimiter}<target_entity>{tuple_delimiter}<relationship_description>{tuple_delimiter}<relationship_strength>)\\n```\\n\\n**\\u5173\\u952e\\u8bbe\\u8ba1\\u7279\\u5f81**\\u5305\\u62ec\\uff1a\\u81ea\\u5b9a\\u4e49\\u5206\\u9694\\u7b26\\u7cfb\\u7edf\\uff08`{tuple_delimiter}`\\u3001`{record_delimiter}`\\uff09\\u786e\\u4fdd\\u8f93\\u51fa\\u53ef\\u9760\\u89e3\\u6790\\uff1b\\u5173\\u7cfb\\u5f3a\\u5ea6\\u4ee5\\u6570\\u503c\\u5206\\u6570\\uff081-10\\uff09\\u8868\\u793a\\uff0c\\u652f\\u6301\\u56fe\\u4e0a\\u7684\\u52a0\\u6743\\u5206\\u6790\\uff1bFew-shot\\u793a\\u4f8b\\u4f7f\\u7528\\u865a\\u6784\\u7684\\\"Verdantis\\\"\\u573a\\u666f\\uff0c\\u907f\\u514d\\u4e0e\\u771f\\u5b9e\\u5b9e\\u4f53\\u6df7\\u6dc6\\u3002\\n\\n**\\u5faa\\u73af\\u62bd\\u53d6\\u673a\\u5236\\uff08Gleaning\\uff09**\\u901a\\u8fc7\\u591a\\u8f6e\\u63d0\\u793a\\u786e\\u4fdd\\u62bd\\u53d6\\u5b8c\\u6574\\u6027\\uff1a\\n```\\nCONTINUE_PROMPT = \\\"MANY entities and relationships were missed in the last extraction. \\nRemember to ONLY emit entities that match any of the previously extracted types. \\nAdd them below using the same format:\\\\n\\\"\\n\\nLOOP_PROMPT = \\\"It appears some entities and relationships may have still been missed. \\nAnswer YES | NO if there are still entities that need to be added.\\\\n\\\"\\n```\\n\\n### \\u58f0\\u660e\\u62bd\\u53d6\\u7684\\u65f6\\u95f4\\u4e0e\\u7f6e\\u4fe1\\u5ea6\\u5efa\\u6a21\\n\\n**claim_extraction\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd**\\u6269\\u5c55\\u4e86\\u5b9e\\u4f53\\u62bd\\u53d6\\u8303\\u5f0f\\uff0c\\u589e\\u52a0\\u4e86\\u65f6\\u95f4\\u7ef4\\u5ea6\\u548c\\u7f6e\\u4fe1\\u5ea6\\u5224\\u65ad\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\nFor each claim, extract the following information:\\n- Subject: name of the entity that is subject of the claim\\n- Object: name of the entity that is object of the claim\\n- Claim Type: overall category of the claim\\n- Claim Status: TRUE, FALSE, or SUSPECTED\\n- Claim Description: Detailed description explaining the reasoning\\n- Claim Date: Period (start_date, end_date) when the claim was made (ISO-8601 format)\\n- Claim Source Text: List of all quotes from the original text relevant to the claim\\n```\\n\\n\\u4e09\\u5143\\u7ec4\\u72b6\\u6001\\u8bbe\\u8ba1\\uff08TRUE/FALSE/SUSPECTED\\uff09\\u652f\\u6301\\u4e0d\\u786e\\u5b9a\\u6027\\u5efa\\u6a21\\uff0c\\u8fd9\\u5728\\u6cd5\\u5f8b\\u3001\\u91d1\\u878d\\u7b49\\u9700\\u8981\\u533a\\u5206\\u5df2\\u786e\\u8ba4\\u4e8b\\u5b9e\\u4e0e\\u7591\\u4f3c\\u4fe1\\u606f\\u7684\\u9886\\u57df\\u5c24\\u4e3a\\u91cd\\u8981\\u3002\\n\\n### \\u793e\\u533a\\u6458\\u8981\\u7684\\u5c42\\u7ea7\\u7b56\\u7565\\n\\n**summarize_descriptions\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd**\\u5904\\u7406\\u540c\\u4e00\\u5b9e\\u4f53\\u7684\\u591a\\u4e2a\\u63cf\\u8ff0\\u7247\\u6bb5\\uff0c\\u8981\\u6c42\\u6a21\\u578b\\u89e3\\u51b3\\u6f5c\\u5728\\u77db\\u76fe\\u5e76\\u751f\\u6210\\u7edf\\u4e00\\u6458\\u8981\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\nYou are a helpful assistant responsible for generating a comprehensive summary \\nof the data provided below.\\nGiven one or more entities, and a list of descriptions, all related to the same \\nentity or group of entities.\\nPlease concatenate all of these into a single, comprehensive description. \\nMake sure to include information collected from all the descriptions.\\nIf the provided descriptions are contradictory, please resolve the contradictions \\nand provide a single, coherent summary.\\n```\\n\\n**\\u793e\\u533a\\u62a5\\u544a\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd**\\u8981\\u6c42\\u751f\\u6210\\u7ed3\\u6784\\u5316\\u7684JSON\\u8f93\\u51fa\\uff0c\\u5305\\u542b\\u6807\\u9898\\u3001\\u6458\\u8981\\u3001\\u5f71\\u54cd\\u4e25\\u91cd\\u6027\\u8bc4\\u5206\\uff080-10\\uff09\\u548c\\u8be6\\u7ec6\\u53d1\\u73b0\\u5217\\u8868\\u3002**Grounding Rules**\\u662fGraphRAG\\u7684\\u72ec\\u7279\\u8bbe\\u8ba1\\uff0c\\u5f3a\\u5236\\u6a21\\u578b\\u5f15\\u7528\\u6570\\u636e\\u6765\\u6e90\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\nPoints supported by data should list their data references as follows:\\n\\\"This is an example sentence supported by multiple data references \\n[Data: <dataset name> (record ids); <dataset name> (record ids)].\\\"\\n\\nDo not list more than 5 record ids in a single reference. Instead, list the top 5 \\nmost relevant record ids and add \\\"+more\\\" to indicate that there are more.\\n```\\n\\n### \\u67e5\\u8be2\\u9636\\u6bb5\\u7684Local/Global/DRIFT\\u4e09\\u6a21\\u5f0f\\n\\n**Local Search\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd**\\u9762\\u5411\\u7cbe\\u786e\\u7684\\u5b9e\\u4f53\\u7ea7\\u67e5\\u8be2\\uff0c\\u76f4\\u63a5\\u4f7f\\u7528\\u7d22\\u5f15\\u6570\\u636e\\u8868\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\n---Role---\\nYou are a helpful assistant responding to questions about data in the tables provided.\\n\\n---Goal---\\nGenerate a response of the target length and format that responds to the user's question, \\nsummarizing all information in the input data tables appropriate for the response length \\nand format, and incorporating any relevant general knowledge.\\n```\\n\\n**Global Search**\\uff08Dynamic Reasoning and Inference with Flexible Traversal\\uff09\\u662f2024\\u5e74\\u5f15\\u5165\\u7684\\u65b0\\u6a21\\u5f0f\\uff0c\\u7ed3\\u5408\\u4e86Local\\u548cGlobal\\u641c\\u7d22\\u7684\\u4f18\\u52bf\\u3002\\u5176Primer\\u9636\\u6bb5\\u63d0\\u793a\\u8bcd\\u8981\\u6c42\\u6a21\\u578b\\u63d0\\u4f9b\\u4e2d\\u95f4\\u7b54\\u6848\\u3001\\u76f8\\u5173\\u6027\\u8bc4\\u5206\\u548c\\u540e\\u7eed\\u67e5\\u8be2\\u5efa\\u8bae\\uff1a\\n\\n```\\nYou will begin your reasoning looking at a summary of the content of the most \\nrelevant communities and will provide:\\n\\n1. score: How well the intermediate answer addresses the query (0-100)\\n2. intermediate_answer: This answer should match the level of detail and length \\n   found in the community summaries. \"TextMemoryPlugin\")\n```\n\n\u5b98\u65b9\u652f\u6301\u7684**Vector Store Connectors**\u5305\u62ec\uff1aAzure AI Two of the most prominent frameworks in 2025 are LangChain and LlamaIndex, each with distinct strengths and use cases. Key features: \u2714 Chain-based workflows \u2013 Enables multi-step LLM interactions \u2714 Agent systems \u2013 Dynamically selects tools (APIs, databases, etc.)\n\nLlamaIndex (formerly GPT Index) is optimized for indexing and retrieving structured/unstructured data to enhance LLM responses via Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). If you need flexible AI pipelines, LangChain is better. For enterprise RAG systems, combining both is ideal.         "text": "Key takeaway up front: LangChain is an orchestration and agent framework (workflows, tools, agents, memory). LlamaIndex is a data-first indexing and retrieval framework (fast RAG, vector indices, flexible connectors).\n\nQuick overviewWhy this distinction matters (short version)Feature breakdown1) Core purpose2) Data & retrieval3) Agents, tools, and workflow4) Integrations and vector stores5) Community, docs, and company supportReal-world use cases where each\u2026\n\nLangChain vs. LlamaIndex (2025): Which AI Framework Should You Choose? ... The LLM ecosystem exploded in 2023\u20132024 and in 2025 it\u2019s matured into a practical toolbox for building real AI apps. If you\u2019re building retrieval-augmented systems, chatbots, or agentic apps, two framework names come up again and again: LangChain and LlamaIndex (formerly GPT Index). Both are battle-tested, popular, and actively developed \u2014 but they solve subtly different problems. This guide helps you choose the right tool for your project by comparing architecture, core features, performance trade-offs, integrations, community adoption, and real-world use cases. I\u2019ll also summarize where teams combine both frameworks for the best of both worlds. Key takeaway up front: LangChain is an orchestration and agent framework (workflows, tools, agents, memory). LlamaIndex is a data-first indexing and retrieval framework (fast RAG, vector indices, flexible connectors).\n\nLangChain \u2014 a developer toolkit for building LLM-powered apps. It provides chains, tools, agents, memory, integrations with LLMs, and an ecosystem for shipping agentic and orchestration workflows. LangChain has grown into an ecosystem with paid products and an open-source core. LlamaIndex \u2014 a data framework focused on turning documents and knowledge sources into queryable indices for LLMs. It specializes in ingestion, indexing (vectors, hierarchical indices), and efficient retrieval for RAG (retrieval-augmented generation) workflows. RAG systems have two main parts: (1) where you store and index knowledge, and what the model does with that knowledge. LlamaIndex is explicitly crafted for \u00b7 (1) \u2014 efficient indexing, vector stores, and retrieval. LangChain specializes in \u00b7 (2) \u2014 chaining LLM calls, connecting tools/APIs, composing agent behaviors, and orchestrating multi-step logic.\n\nLangChain: Orchestration, agents, workflows, memory abstractions. It\u2019s ideal when your app needs to call tools, perform multi-step reasoning, maintain session memory, or combine many LLM calls into a single user flow. Think \u201cagentic\u201d systems and pipelines. LlamaIndex: Data ingestion, indexing, vector stores, and retrieval. It\u2019s purpose-built for converting documents (PDFs, HTML, DBs) into indices that LLMs can query quickly and reliably. LangChain: Has connectors to vector stores (Chroma, Pinecone, FAISS, Milvus). It supports basic indexing workflows, but retrieval logic is usually built by wiring components together. Good for custom retrieval embedded inside larger workflows. LlamaIndex: Comes with rich indexing structures (VectorStoreIndex, TreeIndex, ListIndex, etc.) and first-class support for building and querying indices; optimized for RAG performance and developer ergonomics for document-driven agents.",

The caveat for these debugging capabilities is that most of them live on the LangGraph Server, not the open-source library. No items found. ... This is some text inside of a div block. If you're a developer working on an AI app, you've likely come across LangChain and LlamaIndex. These open source frameworks provide essential tools for building complex AI systems, yet they differ in certain aspects. This article will compare these frameworks in detail, helping you understand their unique features and make informed choices in your AI projects. ... Llamaindex, formerly GPT Index, is an open-source data framework designed to develop powerful context-based LLM applications. Optimized for LLM retrieval tasks, it is great for LLM applications that require integrating user-specific data with LLMs (RAG). Here are some of the key features: Loading: You can load data from 150+ sources in different formats (API\u2019s, PDF\u2019s, docs, SQL..etc) Indexing: You can store and index data in 40+ vector, document, graph, or SQL databas\n\nEvaluations: Recently, Llamaindex joined the evaluation scene, introducing modules for assessing retrieval and response quality. ... Langchain is an open-source framework designed for building end-to-end LLM applications. It provides an extensive suite of components that abstract many of the complexities of building LLM applications. Here are some of the key features: Formatting: You can use components to format user input and LLM outputs using prompt templates and output parsers. Data Handling: You can use various document loaders, text splitters, retrievers, and embedding models. Component Chaining: Using the LangChain Expression Language (LCEL) you can chain all of these components together and build context-aware apps. Additionally, they introduced LlangSmith, so you can trace what\u2019s going on in your chains, and LangServe, which helps you deploy LangChain chains as a REST API.\n\nLangChain, like Llamaindex, offers simple prompt templates for various uses. It's handy for inspiration and starting out. However, with the new LangSmith feature, it now has a basic prompt playground for testing and adding custom templates to your chains, allowing you to separate prompts from your LangChain code. But, it doesn't support comparing multiple prompt versions for various variants yet. Verdict: If you want to begin and experiment with pre-made prompt templates, you\u2019re gonna be good with both options. But if you prefer better prompt organization, Langchain lets you track changes with a full commit history, allowing you to version your prompts and organize them into separate components. If you\u2019re looking for advanced prompt comparison option, you\u2019d be better off with tools like Vellum. ... RAG is a technique that integrates external data with LLMs to improve the quality and relevance of generated outputs.\n\nLlamaIndex offers seamless data indexing and search querying for quick retrieval. It includes abstractions like RetrieverQueryEngine for managing index retrieval pipelines and Settings for integrating different components like embeddings and LLMs. Additionally, it provides SimpleDirectoryReader for handling various data formats and ContextChatEngine for interactive queries through stored document chunks. LangChain is rapidly catching up to LlamaIndex in building RAG applications. You can develop various RAG architectures with Langchain, as it supports all needed components for it. However, Langchain can become very complex to maintain once you start scaling your RAG apps, as users report here. Verdict: Both options are good starting points if you want to build RAG apps. However, if you want to build production-ready RAG, you might lean more towards Llamaindex.",

Select the right framework or combination of frameworks for specific project requirements. This article was published as a part of the Data Science Blogathon. ... You can think of LangChain as a framework rather than just a tool. It provides a wide range of tools right out of the box that enable interaction with large language models (LLMs). A key feature of LangChain is the use of chains, which allow the chaining of components together. For example, you could use a PromptTemplate and an LLMChain to create a prompt and query an LLM. This modular structure facilitates easy and flexible integration of various components for complex tasks. LangChain simplifies every stage of the LLM application lifecycle: Development: Build your applications using LangChain\u2019s open-source building blocks, components, and third-party integrations.\n\nLangChain is highly versatile and adaptable, offering a wide variety of tools for different NLP applications, from simple queries to complex workflows. You can read more about LangChain components here. LlamaIndex (formerly known as GPT Index) is a framework for building context-augmented generative AI applications with LLMs including agents and workflows. Its primary focus is on ingesting, structuring, and accessing private or domain-specific data. LlamaIndex excels at managing large datasets, enabling swift and precise information retrieval, making it ideal for search and retrieval tasks. It offers a set of tools that make it easy to integrate custom data into LLMs, especially for projects requiring advanced search capabilities. LlamaIndex is highly effective for data indexing and querying. Based on my experience with LlamaIndex, it is an ideal solution for working with vector embeddings and RAGs.",

LangChain excels in orchestrating multi-step AI workflows through its modular architecture, while LlamaIndex focuses on optimizing document indexing and retrieval. In 2025, LlamaIndex achieved a 35% boost in retrieval accuracy, making it a top choice for document-heavy applications. Meanwhile, LangChain introduced LangGraph, enhancing workflow control for complex reasoning tasks. For businesses, the choice depends on priorities: LangChain shines in dynamic AI workflows like customer service automation, whereas LlamaIndex is better suited for applications requiring fast and precise document retrieval, such as legal research or technical documentation. LlamaIndex is a platform designed to turn unstructured data into well-organized, searchable knowledge bases. It achieves this through advanced indexing and retrieval techniques, setting itself apart by focusing specifically on document-heavy workflows, unlike broader AI frameworks. One of LlamaIndex's standout strengths is its ability to process a wide range of document formats, such as PDFs, Word files, spreadsheets, and web pages. It combines semantic and keyword-based approaches in hybrid retrieval, uses re-ranking algorithms to improve result relevance, and applies context-aware filtering to consider document relationships. These features make it especially effective for applications where precision is crucial to the user experience. The architectural differences between the two frameworks become apparent in their use cases. Haystack (~5.9 ms) and LlamaIndex (~6 ms) follow, while LangChain (~10 ms) and LangGraph (~14 ms) are higher.\n\nNormalization: A critical step for performance, normalize_embeddings was set to True in all five frameworks. (LangChain/LangGraph via encode_kwargs; LlamaIndex via normalize=True; Haystack via normalize_embeddings; DSPy retriever normalized.) Retrieval: The Qdrant vector store was queried for a k=5 (top 5 documents) in all implementations. ... Web search: The benchmark was restricted to Tavily-only (max_results=3). Calculator: All five implementations used the sympy library for mathematical expression parsing and evaluation, ensuring identical capabilities. The agent\u2019s \u201cdecision-making\u201d process was explicitly mirrored across the board. Routing logic: A hybrid routing strategy was implemented in all five scripts to balance model intelligence with deterministic rules: A regex-based heuristic_route first checks for obvious calculator or web search patterns (e.g., math symbols, years like \u201c2024\u201d).\n\nWe benchmarked 5 RAG frameworks: LangChain, LangGraph, LlamaIndex, Haystack, and DSPy, by building the same agentic RAG workflow with standardized components: identical models (GPT-4.1-mini), embeddings (BGE-small), retriever (Qdrant), and tools (Tavily web search). This isolates each framework\u2019s true overhead and token efficiency. The benchmark consisted of 100 queries, with each framework running the full set 100 times to provide stable averages. ... Avg. Tokens: Total tokens consumed across all LLM calls (router, document grader, answer grader, and generator), includes both prompts (with retrieved context) and completions. Lower = less API cost. Framework Overhead: Pure orchestration time (ms), the framework\u2019s internal processing (routing logic, state management, etc.), excluding LLM API and tool calls. Lower = leaner framework. All implementations achieved 100% accuracy on the test set.\n\nPerformance tracks tokens (under these constraints): DSPy shows the lowest framework overhead (~3.53 ms). Haystack (~5.9 ms) and LlamaIndex (~6 ms) follow, while LangChain (~10 ms) and LangGraph (~14 ms) are higher. Token usage is lowest for Haystack (~1.57k), then LlamaIndex (~1.60k); DSPy and LangGraph are ~2.03k, and LangChain ~2.40k. Routing/tool-path matters: Slight shifts in initial routing (retriever vs. web vs. calculator) and fallback behavior affect both tokens and time, even when prompts and budgets are aligned. Despite standardization, small variances in token counts and latency remain. These are attributable to the inherent, low-level behaviors of each framework, their \u201cDNA.\u201d \u00b7 Prompt & message serialization: Each framework wraps the same logical content with slightly different formatting before sending it to the LLM, creating small but consistent token deltas.\n\nChoose LlamaIndex for: readable, single-file workflows where you value clear procedural logic and easy debugging. LangChain: Imperative with declarative components Orchestration remains a Python script, but individual tasks are small, composable chains using the | operator (e.g., prompt | llm | parser). State is a flexible, untyped Python dict. Choose LangChain for: Rapid prototyping or teams already in the LangChain ecosystem that prefer composing small declarative units within a larger imperative driver. Haystack: Component-based, manual orchestration Typed, reusable components (@component) with explicit I/O, while control flow stays plain Python (if/else). Easy to swap LLM/retriever/web backends, plus first-class per-step instrumentation (external vs. framework time). Choose Haystack for: production-ready, testable pipelines with clear contracts and fine-grained control. DSPy: Signature-first programs (fewer lines of code) Define a task via a signature (inputs/outputs + intent), the",

Me (Phil): Actually, they are not mutually exclusive. You can use DSPy and LlamaIndex or LangChain in one app. However, the philosophy and abstraction of DSPy differ so significantly from that of LlamaIndex and Langchain that it is usually straightforward to decide when DSPy is (or isn\u2019t) the right framework for your use case. From my experience, LangChain and LlamaIndex are amazing libraries for high-level application development, especially when all you need are generic, off-the-shelf prompts for question answering over PDFs or standard text-to-SQL and rich ecosystems for integrations. It allows you to iterate fast on structured code, rather than brittle strings, and offers algorithms that compile AI programs into effective prompts and weights for your language ...\n\nDSPy is a declarative framework for building modular AI software. It allows you to iterate fast on structured code, rather than brittle strings, and offers algorithms that compile AI programs into effective prompts and weights for your language models, whether you're building simple classifiers, sophisticated RAG pipelines, or Agent loops. Instead of wrangling prompts or training jobs, DSPy (Declarative Self-improving Python) enables you to build AI software from natural-language modules and to generically compose them with different models, inference strategies, or learning algorithms. This makes AI software more reliable, maintainable, and portable across models and strategies. tl;dr Think of DSPy as a higher-level language for AI programming (lecture), like the shift from assembly to C or pointer arithmetic to SQL. There are still a number of open issues, especially around security, discovery, trusted deployment, permission control in the MCP protocol. It comes with a open source specification, and a number of SDKs (Python, TypeScript, Java, etc.) to implement MCP Servers and clients. The MCP architecture consists of the MCP Host program which can be configured with a number of MCP Servers that provide capabilities like tools, resources, and prompts. If you want to dive deeper, here is a comprehensive overview with explanations and examples with Neo4j. Whenever a user (or agent) requests in an interaction some task that could be executed by or through using a MCP Server capability, the LLM will select the tool, extract parameters and invoke the tool through an MCP Client connected to the MCP Server.         "text": "Bridging LangChain and MCP requires specialized adapters that translate between the two systems. These adapters handle the complexity of protocol translation, allowing developers to use MCP tools as if they were native LangChain tools.\n\nThe landscape of AI agent development is evolving rapidly, and developers are constantly seeking better ways to build scalable, interoperable systems. Two technologies stand at the forefront of this evolution: LangChain, a powerful framework for building applications with large language models, and the Model Context Protocol (MCP), an emerging standard for tool interoperability. Understanding how to integrate these technologies opens up new possibilities for creating sophisticated AI agents that can seamlessly interact with a vast ecosystem of tools and services. This article explores how integrating LangChain with MCP enables AI agents to access standardized tools, enhance interoperability, and build smarter, scalable workflows. Model Context Protocol represents a fundamental shift in how AI systems discover and interact with tools. Developed and maintained by Anthropic, MCP is an open protocol designed to standardize the way language models access external capabilities, data sources,\n\nLangChain has established itself as the go-to framework for building LLM applications, offering powerful abstractions for chains, agents, and memory management. When you combine LangChain's orchestration capabilities with MCP's standardized tool access, you get the best of both worlds. LangChain handles the complex logic of chaining operations and managing agent behavior, while MCP provides a consistent interface to a growing ecosystem of tools. This synergy brings several benefits to developers. First, it dramatically improves interoperability, allowing LangChain applications to tap into any MCP-compliant tool without writing custom integrations. Second, it enables better scalability, as teams can develop and deploy tools independently while maintaining compatibility. Third, it reduces development time by eliminating the need to rewrite tool integrations for different frameworks.\n\nBridging LangChain and MCP requires specialized adapters that translate between the two systems. These adapters handle the complexity of protocol translation, allowing developers to use MCP tools as if they were native LangChain tools. The langchain-mcp-adapters library serves as the official bridge between LangChain and MCP. Maintained by the LangChain community with contributions from Anthropic, this library provides a seamless integration experience. Installation is straightforward through pip for Python users or npm for TypeScript developers. The library abstracts away the protocol details, exposing a simple API that feels natural to LangChain developers. The library includes comprehensive examples and utilities for common integration patterns. It handles connection management, automatic reconnection, error recovery, and tool schema translation.\n\nThe client handles connection pooling, load balancing, and failover automatically. When a tool is invoked, the client routes the request to the appropriate server, manages the session, and returns the result in a format LangChain understands. Python remains the primary language for LangChain MCP integration, with the most mature SDK and extensive ecosystem support. The Python implementation leverages async/await for efficient concurrent operations and includes type hints for better developer experience. TypeScript support is rapidly improving, catering to the growing number of developers building production applications with Node.js. Cross-language compatibility is a key strength of MCP. Tools written in one language can be consumed by clients in another, thanks to the protocol's language-agnostic design. This flexibility allows teams to choose the best language for each component of their system while maintaining full interoperability.",

        "text": "Rather, it\u2019s an integration layer within an agentic architecture. It complements agent orchestration tools like LangChain, LangGraph, CrewAI, or LlamaIndex by serving as a unified &quot;toolbox&quot; from which AI agents can invoke external actions.\n\nTo simplify that, Anthropic came up with Model Context Protocol (MCP) \u2013 an open standard designed to bridge AI assistants with the world of data and tools, to plug in many different sources of context. They announced it in November 2024. The reaction was sort of blah. But now MCP is trending, already passing Langchain and promising to overcome OpenAPI and CrewAI pretty soon. Major AI players and open-source communities are rallying around MCP, seeing it as a potential game-changer for building agentic AI systems. Why? In this article, we\u2019ll dive deep into MCP \u2013 why it\u2019s a hot topic right now, how MCP enables the shift toward more integrated, context-aware AI, its place in agentic workflows, and the under-the-radar details that developers, researchers, AI engineers, and tech executives should know. We\u2019ll also explore some innovative applications of MCP that few have attempted.\n\nMCP was first open-sourced and announced by Anthropic in late November 2024. At the time, it was an exciting idea but not that many noticed it and took seriously. It\u2019s in early 2025 that MCP has really surged into the AI community\u2019s consciousness. There are a few big reasons for this recent buzz: Integration Problem Solver: AI agents and agentic workflows became major buzzwords in 2023\u20132024, but their Achilles\u2019 heel remained: integrating these agents with real-world business systems and data. Initially, much attention went to model capabilities and prompt techniques, not integration. MCP squarely addresses this gap by defining \u201chow to connect existing data sources\u201d (file systems, databases, APIs, etc.) into AI workflows. As people digested this, MCP started to be seen as the missing puzzle piece for serious, production-ready AI agents.\n\nTool Use via Frameworks (LangChain tools, Agents): Agent orchestration libraries like LangChain popularized the idea of giving models \u201ctools\u201d (functions) with descriptions. For example, you might have a search() tool or a calculate() tool, and the agent (via the LLM) decides when to invoke them. This is powerful, but each tool still required custom implementation under the hood \u2013 LangChain\u2019s library grew to 500+ tools implemented in a consistent interface, yet developers still had to wire up those tools or ensure they fit their needs. MCP can be seen as complementary here: it provides a standardized interface for the implementation of tools. In fact, you can think of MCP servers as a library of ready-made tools that any agent can use. The difference is where the standardization lies. LangChain created a developer-facing standard (its Tool class interface) to integrate tools into an agent\u2019s code.\n\nMCP creates a model-facing standard \u2013 the running AI agent itself can discover and use any MCP-defined tool at runtime. This means even if you don\u2019t custom-build an agent\u2019s code for a particular tool, the model can integrate it on the fly. In practice, these ideas are converging: for example, LangChain\u2019s team (when noticed the surge of MCP) provided an adapter so that all those MCP servers (connectors) can be treated as LangChain tools easily. So an agent built inLLangChain or other frameworks can call MCP tools just like any other, benefiting from the growing MCP ecosystem. Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and Vector Databases: A prevalent way to supply context to LLMs is to use a retriever that searches a knowledge base (documents, embeddings) and injects the top results into the prompt. This addresses the knowledge cutoff or limited memory of models.",

        "is_missing": false,
        "text": "MCP was announced by Anthropic in November 2024 as an open standard for connecting AI assistants to data systems such as content repositories, business management tools, and development environments.\n\nMCP was announced by Anthropic in November 2024 as an open standard for connecting AI assistants to data systems such as content repositories, business management tools, and development environments. It aims to address the challenge of information silos and legacy systems. Before MCP, developers often had to build custom connectors for each data source or tool, resulting in what Anthropic described as an \"N\u00d7M\" data integration problem. Earlier stop-gap approaches\u2014such as OpenAI's 2023 \"function-calling\" API and the ChatGPT plug-in framework\u2014solved similar problems but required vendor-specific connectors. MCP re-uses the message-flow ideas of the Language Server Protocol (LSP) and is transported over JSON-RPC 2.0. In December 2025, Anthropic donated the MCP to the Agentic AI Foundation (AAIF), a directed fund under the Linux Foundation, co-founded by Anthropic, Block and OpenAI, with support from other companies.\n\nThe Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard and open-source framework introduced by Anthropic in November 2024 to standardize the way artificial intelligence (AI) systems like large language models (LLMs) integrate and share data with external tools, systems, and data sources. MCP provides a universal interface for reading files, executing functions, and handling contextual prompts. Following its announcement, the protocol was adopted by major AI providers, including OpenAI and Google DeepMind. MCP was announced by Anthropic in November 2024 as an open standard for connecting AI assistants to data systems such as content repositories, business management tools, and development environments. You can use MCP servers with local models through frameworks like LangChain or LlamaIndex. The quality of tool usage will depend on the model's capabilities - models like Llama 3.3 70B or Qwen 2.5 72B handle MCP tools well, while smaller models may struggle with complex tool selection. Technically yes, but it's not recommended. You'd need to implement the MCP client protocol yourself, handle tool discovery, manage message passing, and deal with error handling. The frameworks in this guide handle all of this complexity for you. Even lightweight options like the OpenAI Agents SDK are easier than rolling your own. Start by checking the MCP server logs - most servers provide detailed logging. Common issues include authentication failures (check your environment variables), network connectivity (ensure the host is reachable), and timeout errors (increase connection timeout).\n\nEach framework has different strengths: Claude Agent SDK for security-first production, OpenAI Agents SDK for delegation patterns, CrewAI for multi-agent workflows, LangChain for ecosystem breadth, Agno for minimal code, DSPy for prompt optimization, and more. We have a look at each of the frameworks and include code for interacting with a Model Context Protocol (MCP) Server for each of them. Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open protocol that standardizes how applications interact with large language models (LLMs). Launched in late 2024, MCP has become the de facto standard for integrating systems and applications with LLMs, with support now available across major AI platforms, including OpenAI, Gemini, and Google's Vertex AI. Platforms like GitHub, AWS and ClickHouse build MCP servers, which define sets of tools and resources that LLMs can interact with.\n\n\ud83d\udcc4 View the full LangChain example \ud83e\uddea Try the LangChain notebook \u00b7 LlamaIndex (formerly GPT Index) specializes in data-aware AI applications, particularly those involving retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). While other frameworks focus on general agent capabilities, LlamaIndex is optimized for scenarios where agents need to work with large amounts of structured and unstructured data. LlamaIndex's MCP integration is designed to work seamlessly with its data indexing and retrieval capabilities. This makes it particularly powerful when combined with analytical databases like ClickHouse - you can use MCP tools to query structured data, then combine those results with LlamaIndex's document retrieval and synthesis capabilities to provide comprehensive answers.\n\n\ud83d\udcc4 View the full LlamaIndex example \ud83e\uddea Try the LlamaIndex notebook \u00b7 PydanticAI launched in December 2024 by the team behind the popular Pydantic validation library. It brings Pydantic's philosophy of type safety and data validation to the world of AI agents. If you've ever struggled with agents returning inconsistently formatted data or making type errors when calling tools, PydanticAI aims to solve these problems. PydanticAI's MCP integration leverages Pydantic's powerful validation system to ensure that all inputs and outputs from MCP servers conform to expected schemas. This means runtime type checking for all MCP tool calls, automatic validation of responses, and clear error messages when something goes wrong. It's particularly valuable when building agents that need to integrate with existing Python applications that already use Pydantic for data validation.",

With Haystack, the integration often centers on document stores and pipelines. LlamaIndex indices can be converted into Haystack-compatible formats using utilities like CompatibilityGraphStore, which maps LlamaIndex\u2019s graph structures to Haystack\u2019s document nodes. For instance, after creating a KnowledgeGraphIndex in LlamaIndex, you can export its nodes and relationships to a Haystack InMemoryDocumentStore. From there, Haystack pipelines can use retrievers like EmbeddingRetriever or custom components to process queries.\n\nIntegrating LlamaIndex with libraries like LangChain and Haystack involves connecting its specialized indexing and retrieval capabilities to broader workflow frameworks. LlamaIndex excels at organizing unstructured data into searchable structures, while LangChain focuses on chaining LLM-related tasks, and Haystack provides pipelines for document processing. The integration typically revolves around passing data between these tools using their native interfaces or adapters. For LangChain integration, start by using LlamaIndex to create a structured index of your data (e.g., documents, PDFs). LangChain\u2019s LlamaIndexRetriever wrapper lets you treat a LlamaIndex query engine as a LangChain retriever. For example, after building an index with LlamaIndex\u2019s VectorStoreIndex, you can initialize a RetrieverQueryEngine and pass it to LangChain\u2019s RetrievalQA chain.\n\nThis allows LangChain to handle tasks like conversation history or tool orchestration while leveraging LlamaIndex\u2019s efficient data retrieval. LlamaIndex indices can be converted into Haystack-compatible formats using utilities like CompatibilityGraphStore, which maps LlamaIndex\u2019s graph structures to Haystack\u2019s document nodes. For instance, after creating a KnowledgeGraphIndex in LlamaIndex, you can export its nodes and relationships to a Haystack InMemoryDocumentStore. From there, Haystack pipelines can use retrievers like EmbeddingRetriever or custom components to process queries.\n\nA practical use case might involve using LlamaIndex to build a domain-specific knowledge graph, LangChain to manage user interactions and context, and Haystack to deploy the system as an API endpoint. The key is to let each tool handle its strengths: LlamaIndex for optimized retrieval, LangChain for task coordination, and Haystack for pipeline scalability. The tool-calling agent, represents the most recent and versatile implementation of Agent, capable of supporting a wide variety of LLM providers such as OpenAI, Anthropic, Google Gemini, Mistral, and others.\n\nCombining Langchain and LlamaIndex to build your first Agentic RAG System Building Large Language Model (LLM) applications can be tricky, especially when we are deciding between different frameworks \u2026\n\nBuilding Large Language Model (LLM) applications can be tricky, especially when we are deciding between different frameworks such as Langchain and LlamaIndex. While LlamaIndex excels at intelligent search and data retrieval, LangChain is a more versatile and general-purpose framework for LLM applications, offering greater compatibility with a wide range of platforms. But what if we could combine them, leveraging the unique strengths of both frameworks to address our specific needs? This blog is a starting guide on how to integrate LlamaIndex and LangChain to create a scalable and customizable Agentic RAG application. Before we move forward with the implementation, let\u2019s take a moment to understand the essence of an Agentic RAG. An Agentic RAG refers to an Agent-based RAG implementation. It is an advancement over the Naive RAG approach, adding autonomous behavior and enhancing decision-making capabilities.\n\nStep 4: We convert the LlamaIndex Tools into a format compatible with Langchain Agents. #convert to langchain format llamaindex_to_langchain_converted_tools = [t.to_langchain_tool() for t in query_engine_tools] We also define an additional Langchain Tool with Web Search functionality. After that, we combine all tools. #Another Langchain Tool search = DuckDuckGoSearchRun() duckduckgo_tool = Tool( name='DuckDuckGoSearch', func= search.run, description='Use for when you need to perform an internet search to find information that another tool can not provide.' ) langchain_tools = [duckduckgo_tool] #Combine to create final list of tools tools = llamaindex_to_langchain_converted_tools + langchain_tools \u00b7 Step 5: We\u2019ll initialize Langchain\u2019s latest Tool Calling Agent. The tool-calling agent, represents the most recent and versatile implementation of Agent, capable of supporting a wide variety of LLM providers such as OpenAI, Anthropic, Google Gemini, Mistral, and others.\n\nSince this information is out-of-scope for any of the retriever tools, the agent correctly decided to invoke the external search tool. It\u2019s worth noting that this setup represents a fundamental Agentic RAG configuration, with potential for increased complexity. For instance, rather than equipping a single agent with multiple tools, we can introduce multiple agents, each specialized in a subset of documents within the same domain. At the helm, a top-level agent would serve as a supervisor for these agents. You can find the complete implementation from my notebook here.
